There are, of course, still problems in this area despite the undisputed recognition of child protection and children's rights. In 2010 alone, a total of 522 cases of a body injury against an unmistakable victim were reported. In almost half of the cases, the children were even younger than ten years old. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and BZÖ.]] In the case of violent offences against children which are not yet subject to minimum penalties, such penalties shall therefore be provided for. Where there are already minimum penalties, these will be increased in the future. Furthermore, the use of violence to children should be considered as a particular source of difficulty. In particular, it is a signal to stress that this injustice is simply not tolerated against the children. The objection of overreaction or a lack of classification is not convincing. It is precisely about placing the child's well-being at the centre not only in the area of raising the maximum penalty, but also in establishing the criminal framework and ensuring the child's well-being in criminal law. If it is now occasionally claimed that the simple blow on the butt of an unmistakable child is threatened with a minimum sentence of two months, then this will leave the seriousness of the argumentation. Apart from the fact that this clause is not covered by the provision of § 39a of the Criminal Code, because it is an abuse and not violence, the whole range of attitudes still remains available, due to poverty, different measures for cases below the scale of suspense, to extraordinary punishment. That is to say, it is always left to the judicial assessment in the individual case, which sentence is applied as appropriate for action and indebtedness. High house! I have talked about protecting children and young people from new dangers. However, we must not overlook the fact that the Internet also poses dangers for children and young people. In recent years, it has increasingly happened that full-year-olds are trying to make sexual contacts with children and young people via the Internet. Often, full-year-olds come out as younger people, get into contact with the unmistakable over the Internet, build trust and then agree on a real meeting. Unfortunately, sexual assaults occur very often at these real meetings. This is where we want to prevent. We want to prevent these sexual assaults from happening at all, so we already have to punish the start of sexual contacts. A new crime, namely the prohibition of so-called grooming, is therefore to be created. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In addition, the scientific consideration of a live broadcast of pornographic performances by minors, for example, should be punished via webcams on the Internet. Here, too, a two-year sentence threatens. Finally, the list of criminal offences committed abroad, for which the Austrian criminal laws apply without regard to the laws at the crime scene, including the genital mutilation or the forced marriage, should also be extended. The Austrian criminal law now applies to all these offences, even if this act is committed abroad, provided that the perpetrator or victim of Austrian citizens is or has his usual residence in Austria. It is really about being able to do even more effectively, even better, against such crimes as forced marriage or genital mutilations that are being committed abroad in Austria. They're afraid of having to testify against their relatives. You're afraid of even standing up as an accused, and that's the biggest problem. Experts say that there may even be difficulties in using help from experts, with professional support. In my opinion, this also poses the greatest danger. Violence and sexual assaults not only cause physical traces and physical damage, but they are associated with enormous psychological strains that can change the whole of life. Therefore, all measures – including those we are taking today – are not only measures that change the matter of law and, of course, include handling, but they do not detract us from the obligation that we should also look and point out that we must encourage the victims and also the witnesses to show offenses, abuses, abuses in order to be able to take advantage of aid. We know that victims in later life can also become perpetrators, that they may also be able to use violence in their own families. Therefore, there is zero tolerance for violence against children!

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e15766	Mrs Bundesminister Dr. Karl spoke on a point of view. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e15788	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! This debate is about the amendment of three very different laws, namely the amendment of the Federal Law on judicial cooperation in criminal matters with the Member States of the European Union, the so-called EU-JZG, to the amendment of the Labour and Social Courts Act and the amendment of the Law on Criminal Organisation. Let me take a brief look at all three templates. The amendment of the EU-JZG is aimed in particular at implementing the EU Framework Decision on the application of the principle of mutual recognition to judgments in criminal matters, which imposes a fine or measure that is withdrawn from freedom. This Framework Decision, which also took place at the initiative of Austria, pursued the objective that the resocialisation opportunities of prisoners after detention are much better if the penalty is carried out in their home country. That is to say, it should also be facilitated in the resocialisation of the convicted person. The proposed amendment therefore allows nationals from other EU Member States to lay down sentences of freedom to which they have been sentenced domestically, i.e. in Austria, in their home country. That means it really gets too much relief here. Conversely, this also applies to Austrian citizens, which is clearly to be said. It is also necessary to say that this is already possible under applicable law, but it will be facilitated and simplified in so far as no further consent of the executing State and the convict is required in the future if the convicted person has the nationality of the executing State and is either resident in that State or would be postponed there following the end of the sentence. If the defendant does not have the nationality of the executing State, but has been legally resident there for at least five years, consent of the convicted person is still necessary. If the aforementioned circumstances – i.e. citizenship et cetera – do not exist, the consent of both the executing State and the convict is required. If such consent is given, the execution may also take place in the executing State, whenever there is a special commitment of the convicted to the executing State. This particular bond can be family or economic in nature, because it is assumed that the resocialisation opportunities are simply increased by the execution of the detention in that state, if a family bond exists, an economic bond. The costs shall be borne by the Member State in which the detention is carried out. Let me give you an example: For example, if a Dutch national is sentenced in Austria, the penalty can be imposed in the Netherlands in the future. The costs shall be borne by the Netherlands. Only the transfer costs to the Netherlands are to be taken over from Austria. These newly created opportunities are intended to lead to a discharge of Austrian law enforcement in the EU's foreign country as nationals of other EU Member States in the Austrian criminal system. And, of course, this discharge of the Austrian penitentiary also leads to a discharge of the judicial budget. I am very pleased, Mr Fichtenbauer, that the FPÖ has now come to this knowledge and will give its approval here. The second amendment to this Act concerns an amendment to the Labour and Social Courts Act. This is about § 93 ASG. In § 93 para. 1 ASG is intended to cover the costs of those procedures in social security matters in which a social security institution is involved. To date, this scheme has been managed in such a way that the main association of the Austrian social security funds has made a lump sum. ==References====External links== ==References====External links== However, the actual costs incurred in social law proceedings involving the participation of a social security institution in 2011 already amount to € 53 million, i.e. there is a difference of €12 million. This proposal for a law is now intended to replace the actual costs incurred by the judicial authorities. It was asked why entry into force is postponed to 2013. If you look at the government template we had in the committee and the related materials, it is clear that entry into force only with 1. ==References====External links== This is due on the one hand to § 98 para. 26 but also very clearly from the explanations, where the second sentence in the last paragraph reads: ‘The planned changeover will be applied for the first time in 2013.’ Why not in 2013? – The declaration is to be seen in the fact that the Federal Financial Framework Act would otherwise have to be amended, and in order not to have to change the Federal Financial Framework Act, it will not enter into force until 2013. Only then will the actual costs be replaced. [[Abg. Scheibner: That means you do not give up to €12 million!]] The Federal Financial Framework Act should have been amended. We agreed that this new method of calculation, this new method of payment will take place from 2013. But that was, as has been said, already so in the judicial committee, it was now only made clearer and clearer. The third amendment to which it is now concerned is a change to the court organisation law. The legal organisation law is to be amended to further improve the judicial organisation. This is a matter of various measures. It has already been mentioned that there should be a so-called pre-registration system in the future during the judicial days. The purpose is to improve the organisation and handling of court days by means of a pre-registration system. I say quite clearly here: It is not about the days of office that take place at the courts, but about the court days that take place in places where there is no court place. When you talk to judges, you will hear again and again that they have to go to court days, where they are hardly frequented, that it is rare or very rare that citizens or parties actually come to the court day. Therefore, this pre-login system. In future, it will be possible to better organise these court days. The prior application may also be supported by IT. This will also ensure better civil service, because if there are several notifications, waiting times can be reduced, and better preparation can also be made for discussions with citizens and the parties. This is really a more targeted service for citizens, but of course it is also a contribution to economic efficiency and economy. This is also the intention of this measure. Another measure which I would like to address in connection with the amendment of the Law on Court Organisation concerns the legal specification of the judicial offices established by the four Supreme Courts. The basic idea of these internal contact points is the realization of unbureaucratic, rapid and modern management of complaints. Experience has shown that the judicial ombudsmen actually meet the needs of citizens of qualified contacts. In fact, there is a very large demand for the population, in particular for explanations of court decisions taken, but also for explanations of how individual proceedings were taken, why individual procedural steps have been taken. It really must be said that this consultation and information by the judicial ombudspersons is being carried out very quickly. On average, within two weeks, the completion of the requests will ensure a comprehensive and competent examination of the concerns in addition to the rapid treatment. And now these judicial ombudsmen are also to be laid down by law. The third point I would like to address is a legal anchorage. If we are talking about a good court organization, it is of course also about ensuring good working conditions for the employees. In this context, the association of Austrian judges is a very important task. The association of the Austrian judges has already actually exercised a right of participation, was in fact already very active in the field of participation, now it is to be granted a legal right of participation. This legal right of participation, which is granted to the association of Austrian judges, refers to the safeguarding of the interests of judges when it comes to, for example, important changes in the service. The association of the Austrian judges is granted a right of hearing and consultation. They can also make proposals and deliver opinions. All that has already been done, in fact, is now to be laid down by law. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] You see, a wide range of measures, and I am pleased that this wide range of measures is now in place for the vote. Thank you.

We implement here in the association law: that institutions of associations are not liable for non-refundable activity in the event of slight negligence, neither to the association nor to third parties, outsiders. They are still liable for gross negligence and, of course, for intent, but not for slight negligence. The claims made from the outside must then be taken over by the association. It is very important that it is also stated that liability insurance of the association must also include such claims to institutions. I therefore believe that an important further step will be taken to make this step towards voluntary engagement easier for the many people involved in associations in Austria, and to make it easier for them to act as an institution, as an observer, whether or not they are responsible for an association. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e16311	Mrs Bundesminister Dr. Karl spoke on a point of view. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e16332	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Of course, I am very pleased that the association law Novelle 2011 meets such broad approval. As you know, there has been a discussion on the simplification of liability in this area for a long time, and this discussion has been triggered by the so-called judo case. A beginner was injured in judo training by a club member by a too hard throw, too fast throw. In this case, the Supreme Court confirmed the liability of the association, which had committed itself contractually to carrying out the training of the applicant. This judgment of the Supreme Court has led to a major uncertainty within the associations. In particular, the fear was that now club members will be liable if they have not completed liability insurance for the association. It was therefore repeatedly demanded by the associations that the risk of liability be reduced by voluntary association officials. It has already been pointed out by previous speakers that this year, in 2011, we are celebrating the “European Year of Voluntaryness”, and what would be better than this year to finally comply with the wishes of the associations and to facilitate liability for non-refundable club officials, based on German association law? According to the new § 24 para. 1 of the Association Act shall be excluded from the liability of a non-refundable organ administrator or auditor to the Association in the event of slight negligence. This means that in the future there will only be more liability for gross negligence and purpose. Thus, if, for example, an auditor is slightly negligent by a lack of attention to irregularities in the examination and thus causes damage to the association, the auditor is not liable to the association. Another example: If the associationsobmann refuses to take out liability insurance for the association, although this would be necessary due to the activity of the association, and then a damage to the association due to non-insurance-covered claims and there is only slight negligence, even if the associationobmann is not liable in this case. In this connection, I would also like to mention the new paragraph. 5 in § 24 of the Association Act. This is because the action of a non-refundable club official does not damage the association, but rather is a third party. It was also important for us to ensure that the damaged third party does not fall within the scope of his claims for damages. This means, of course, that the injured third party is to be replaced by its damage, but not by the non-refundable association official, but by the association itself. This means, for example, that if the injured third party is interested in the damaging club owner or auditor, then he can hold the association unharmed and unchallenged if he is accused only of slight negligence. I would also like to give an example of this. If, for example, an association event does not take sufficient security measures – think of lock-offs – and a guest is injured at this event, but there is only slight negligence and this guest wants to claim his claims for damages against the club owner, then the club owner can hold himself in a bad and unchallenged manner at the club. This regulation ensures that the injured guest, the injured guest, is very likely to be satisfied with his claims, by the association and not by the non-refundable club owner. Generally not covered by this new regulation are claims for damages against mere club members, i.e. only club members remain liable. Since this is not the case, however, one must of course maintain that in such cases, if the member of the association is active free of charge, a lower level of care will be applied. Finally, it is also new in the association law that the term of office or convocation of the general meeting is changed from currently four to five years. The reason for this is to reduce the organisational, human and financial effort resulting from the fact that a general meeting is to be convened at least every four years. In the future, it is sufficient to convene such a meeting at least every five years. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen, I believe that we are once again setting up an important measure to strengthen the Office of Honor, and thank you for your attention!

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e16650	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen! As the previous speaker has already said, this is about a federal law with which the Motor Vehicle Liability Insurance Act, the Railway and Motor Vehicle Liability Act and other laws such as the Piper or Gas Economics Act should be amended. The starting point of this amendment is the implementation of the EU Directive on Motor Vehicle Liability Insurance, which provides for an adjustment of the minimum amount of coverage for personal and property damage every five years. In principle, these minimum coverage amounts are based on the European consumer price index. In Austria, it is practice and, as I believe, good practice that minimum insurance amounts are above those of the European provisions. This means that if someone is injured or is tragically killed, there is a minimum amount of coverage, which has been increased from €5 million to €5.6 million. In case of damage to property, the minimum coverage amount was increased from € 1 million to € 1.12 million. This corresponds to an increase in these minimum coverage amounts of at least 12 percent. I believe that this means greater and better protection for the Austrians. Increased premiums are not to be expected as the insurance companies have already completed a large part of the cover amount contracts. I believe that we should jointly decide on this increased protection for consumers. Mr Scheibner, you have not completely convinced us. I believe that it is a sensible implementation, and thank you for your approval.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e16688	Mrs Bundesminister Dr. Karl spoke on a point of view. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e16709	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! It has already been pointed out that Directive 2009/103 on motor vehicle liability insurance provides for an adjustment of the required minimum amount of coverage for personal or property damage to inflation. As a result, we now increase the minimum amount of insurance in § 9 of the Motor Vehicle Liability Insurance Act from 1. ==References== Since then, the minimum amounts relevant to Austrian law have been higher than those prescribed by Community law. This tradition is also to continue with the proposed draft, in the interests of those who are injured and insured in Austria. As far as your concerns are concerned, Mr Scheibner, I would like to stress that the proposed measure is very friendly. A higher amount of coverage is available to the victims of the accident in the event of major damage. It is also true that the majority of the holders of the authorisation have already voluntarily signed higher amounts of insurance and that the increase in insurance amounts by an average of 17 percent only entails marginal premium increases. Due to this wireless radio technology, for example, the customer in the supermarket does not notice whether a product is fitted with this chip or not and whether and when the information will be read out. The location of a product can be tracked worldwide, so to speak, and the customer, the consumer, has no way to control how often this data is read out of this chip and into which databases these data are then transferred. Consumer protectors therefore require, for example, labelling all products equipped with such a chip. There's no advantage without any disadvantage, but that's exactly what it is. What is helpful in logistics is often opposed to data protection. We have already submitted an application concerning the labelling of the products fitted with such a chip in 2009. This request was made on 12 December. November 2009, with the argument that there are two years to implement this within the meaning of consumer and data protection within the European Union. ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e19212	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! At the last meeting of the Consumer Protection Committee, we discussed a number of requests. I would now like to make a few comments on the proposal which deals with heavy metals in jewellery. Cheap jewelry meets us almost everywhere, in the supermarket, in fashion houses, in drugstore markets and other outlets. The main customers for this jewelry are mainly young people, but especially children. Although consumers generally consider these jewels to be safe, international investigations show that most of these products are contaminated with heavy metals, and mainly with heavy metal lead. There are heavy metal guidelines for children's toys, but not for children's jewelry. That is why we see the need for action in this regard, since small amounts of lead are already classified as very worrying. The lead intake via the intestine is also stronger in children than in adults. Lead in the body has a half-life of 30 years, and it has also been shown that already low doses lead to impairments, such as intelligence, reaction or attention, behavioral disorders are also detected. We therefore support this proposal, the European rules on limit values to be available in the spring of next year. The Product Safety Advisory Board will also publish recommendations this year, of which we assume that they will receive sufficient attention from the customers.

This means whether or not a disease that occurs on a professional basis is recognized or not has an impact on the wife and the man who suffers from it. I can call you other diseases. Many women, especially women who work in care, have massive problems in the support system and in the muscle apparatus, because they are permanently wearing, especially removing old people. This too is not recognised, and this is just an example. This list is to be continued for chemicals to which women are exposed, for example, hairdressers. Or in the hospitality: There has been a request from colleague Johann Maier on health damage caused by noise in discotheques (). There, too, many women work as waitresses at the bar. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Hörl.]] Yeah, but they go there voluntarily, the others work there. It is not to be seen if they suffer from an overly high level of noise from a occupational disease because they have to work there. Mr President, look at this! I believe that women are disadvantaged in this area. It is a pity that you reject this request.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e23845	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! High house! As mentioned above, the present railway legislation implements various EU directives, which also serve to harmonise the technical differences of the railways in Europe. Railways must also become more attractive across the EU in terms of freight transport. Various trackways, different train safety systems, which are not compatible or interoperable, i.e. are not suitable for continuous and safe trains, different electricity systems and, last but not least, different safety standards are technical differences which are still making Europe-wide transport difficult by rail. The amendment to be adopted today will enable the necessary harmonisation. The long-term objective is to bring the European rail network into a denominator technically and safely. Each state has its own opportunities at national level, and we also contribute to it. Our goal is to maintain the high technical level and also to maintain the high share of the power supply of the railways through the ÖBB power plants. This will make the railway much cheaper to the ÖBB-own electricity than if it would have to be bought on expensive terms. A sale would be a loss business. Rather, the emphasis must be placed on continuing to work towards the competitiveness of the railways over the road despite budget consolidation. ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e25194	Ladies and gentlemen! Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, In 2009, there were 190 criminal investigations on trafficking in human beings by the criminal police in Austria, and in only 15 cases, a humanitarian right was granted for humanitarian reasons, because someone has become victims of trafficking in human beings. This alone shows that the protection of victims does not work with the current legal provisions, because, in 190 cases, the criminal police are determined, but only 15 victims of trafficking in human beings are helped, only in 15 cases are prevented that a deportation takes place, so this also means that the victims of trafficking in human beings are generally without protection and that, therefore, the perpetrators, the traffickers, in most cases, are free and unpunished and can unfortunately continue to live up to their criminal business. For this very reason, I have put forward this proposal, with the aim and purpose of improving the protection of victims of trafficking in human beings and of better persecution and punishment of the perpetrators, the traffickers and the traders of human beings. You may all remember: There was a prominent case in June 2011, so this is not a long time ago: There was a young Nigerian woman who had the courage to contact the Austrian authorities, cooperate with the police and with the justice so that her people's dealers can be punished, this young woman was transferred to Nigeria as a "reward" – under quotation marks. And we now know by the EXIT association, who also contacted this young woman after her deportation and tried to continue to care for her that she was found in Nigeria by her people's dealers and was finally forced to save her life, to subdue in Nigeria. She is now probably still in Nigeria somewhere and is trying to protect her life from the people they have brought to Austria, who have taken them to the forced prostitution, which they have exploited for many years here, who have earned a lot of money over their forced prostitution and who, as I said, are free to run around as free citizens in that country. For the procedure that had been opened because this young woman had the courage to contact the police and the justice, this procedure was terminated by the prosecutor. And that's not an individual case, and we know it's not an individual case. Our current legal provisions do not offer protection to the victims of trafficking in human beings, even in cases where women – and in most cases Cases are women – they have the courage to go to the authorities and talk about their dealers. It is precisely for this reason that we have requested this request that a one-year right of residence be granted not only in the event of a procedure being opened and when the traffickers are punished, but in all cases where it is found that a person has become a victim of trafficking in human beings. And after this time of thought, when the victim decides to cooperate with the authorities in order to allow its dealers to be put in court, a red-and-white red card is to be issued. Unfortunately, we regret that there was no majority in the Human Rights Committee for this proposal, because this means that the victims of trafficking in human beings are still free-wild, will not continue to trust the authorities to turn to justice to ensure that the criminal perpetrators they have committed to forced prostitution can be punished. I therefore ask you to reconsider your decision to allow the victims of trafficking in human beings in Austria to be finally protected. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e26674	Mr President. Minister! High house! To date, a serious debate on pre-implantation diagnostics policy has not yet taken place. Pre-implantation diagnostics are permitted in almost all EU countries except Ireland. The study is a genetic examination for the recognition of certain dispositions of the embryo in the context of in vitro fertilization before the embryo is transferred to the womb. This makes it possible to detect inherited family diseases, chromosome disorders and other severe disorders. Pre-implantation diagnostics should only be used in couples who are at risk of having children with disabilities or severe diseases due to genetic disposition. The decision whether the egg cell or embryo is then planted or not lies solely with the affected couple. But no one can take this decision. The Committee strongly approved this request by all parties. Why this request is then forwarded to the Committee on Justice without taking a decision is a mystery to me. For me it looks more like you're afraid of your own courage to decide something without perhaps having the population on its side. An important part of these legal provisions is for me that technical training in the establishments is now also adapted to the provisions of the Vocational Training Act and is carried out by professional persons. That is why this law is for me a milestone in training in dental and dental assistance. This training can withstand any comparison between the various health professions in the national and international sectors. The transitional provisions – this is important to me – also ensure that the trainees gain professional qualifications and that this professional qualification is very important for the so-called professional protection. I am glad that this legal provision has been adopted unanimously in committee. Dear ladies and gentlemen! They often resemble known substances in their effect, but have changed chemical structures and can thus be legally distributed. Legally, however, does not mean harmless. Adverse reactions, risks, long-term consequences are not calculable. And in combination with other illegal substances or alcohol, this results in a dangerous cocktail with often heavy poisonings. With minor changes in the molecular structure, new drugs are being created again and again to avoid the legal bans – for the authorities, an powerless situation, for consumers, there may be a deadly risk. Neither the law on pharmaceuticals nor the legislation on addiction provide sufficient resources to successfully combat producers and dealers. Therefore, the New Psychoactive Substances Act will now close this gap by defining whole groups of substances. Together with monitoring, rapid market monitoring and evaluation will be possible. In any event, I think that the application will mean more support for the work of the authorities and more safety for consumers.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e28263	Mrs Mag. Aubauer is now speaking. - Please.

Just how that works, that's exactly what my previous speaker described in detail. It is important to respond to market changes very quickly and very quickly there will be bans on such drugs. ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00135_d8e30085	Madam President. High house! After the Court of Auditors has been referred to several times in connection with the implementation of the recommendations, I would like to point out: There are also three follow-up reviews on today's agenda. If you look at the outcome of the follow-up reviews, you can see that in those areas – this has been addressed by Mrs Schönpass – where no system or competence changes are required, the degree of implementation is very high: as you have said, 83 percent of recommendations are implemented. However, if you look at those areas like family-related services – if you take them as an example or the weather services – you can see that, due to the necessary cooperation of local authorities, due to the need to carry out competence adjustments, there is no evidence of any reform that is necessary in order to actually make Austria financially future-oriented. It has already been discussed in the context of the debate and, among other things, by Mr Schönpass and Mr Gahr, we need reforms in family-related services, in order to be able to give an answer to what kind of support a family has received, and at the same time to be able to pay this in a targeted manner. This is not the case at present, since there is not even a clear definition of what a family is. There is also no clear definition of what one understands as family-related benefits. In addition, the requirements for entitlements, such as social need, family income et cetera, are defined differently. In addition, the execution is divided into seven ministries and several departments in the countries. The use of different basis of assessment, such as student aid or study aid, which also depends on the type of school, whether the Federal Ministry of Education and Art, the Council of State, the Council of Town Schools or the Heads of State or Government, shows that there are regulatory deficits that should be eliminated. In the case of weather services, which Mrs Lapp has referred to, Mrs Lapp has to mention: Weather services have grown. But unfortunately in this case the costs have also increased. I will only briefly carry out that a reform project should not happen in the same way that weather services have failed in the 25 years. This is a reform project which has become self-evident. It is only briefly mentioned how it should not be done; That has already been done. In 1986, i.e. under my predecessor, consideration has already been given to setting up the weather services. The result was an administrative agreement, in 1988 the oral commitment of those responsible to put the weather services together. A study was commissioned in 1990: Saving potential in the merger: 43 million schilling. In 1991, the Council of Ministers decided to merge the weather services and set up a working group, which set out: 58 million schilling potential. In 1995, the ZAMG, the Central Institute for Meteorology and Geodynamics, presented a study with a saving volume of 50 to 60 million schilling. 1997 the following study: Saving potential from 1998 to 2002: € 13 million. 2002 again a business plan: Saving potential €35 million. 2003: the government programme anchored the restructuring of the weather services. There is a working group in 2004, according to which there is a saving volume of €9.4 million from 2004 to 2009. 2004 has failed to restructure; The result is: One doubles the number of employees for the commercial sector to 101 employees in the sub-licensed area of the ZAMG. [[Abg. Neugebauer: Summary!]] Of course there is a counter-reaction: In the field of air traffic weather services, the ACG forms its own company, which is now also active in the commercial field, and both public bodies compete for orders from other undertakings such as ASFINAG. There is an administrative agreement in 2011 as well as in 1986. Now consider the summary: From 2004 to 2009, there would have been a savings potential of €9.4 million, but in fact there were additional costs due to the non-combination of €13.4 million. The savings potential was missed by € 22.8 million. The ZAMG and the ACG compete at the same time when it comes to participating in the market, while at the same time affecting free competition due to their position. The ZAMG also competes with the meteorological institutes at the universities. The research contracts were no longer awarded to the universities, but € 4.4 million went to the ZAMG to finance the sub-licensed area. That means these funds missed the universities. At the same time, the ZAMG had very low revenue by expanding the range of tasks compared to increasing staff costs. In addition, the proportion of flight meteorology in total air safety costs in Austria is 50 percent higher than in the EUROCONTROL average. An example that shows that it would certainly have been appropriate to start a reform earlier: The average cost for an austro-control employee is €127,000, at the MeteoServe, which was founded in 2006 and is almost the same as in the central area of ACG, €35 000. It shows: It pays off making reforms. It pays off to stand up to the reforms, and if one fails to implement reforms, it will cost more than it does. That is why I can only appeal – with regard to the debt brakes, with regard to the requirements of consolidation – that one takes the reforms seriously and actually raises the potential that lies in the system. We will need this to make Austria fit for the future. The Court of Auditors is available to you as a consultant, but the responsibility is certainly with you, within the framework of the fact that a reorganisation of competence is necessary and, accordingly, legal regulations are essential. - Thank you.
